# Literature lead: terminal storage for retained certificate history

Source screen by main, 2026-09-26. This is a bounded research lead, not a theorem import or a correctness judgment. The papers were opened as standard methodology sources, not searched as posted solutions to the exact benchmark. The selected current shared files and SOURCE_NOTES.md had no matches for the searched IQC/OZF terms; that is not an exhaustive novelty claim.

## Primary sources and checked boundaries

[Scherer (2022), arXiv:2207.01363](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2207.01363), especially Theorem 11, equations (25), (30)--(35), Appendix A.5 and section 7. The paper constructs discrete-time constraints with a quadratic terminal term for convex subgradients, using causal and anticausal filters. A finite doubly hyperdominant matrix condition certifies the construction. The displayed filter starts at zero. The stability theorem also needs a coupled storage positivity condition. Its proposed losslessness relative to a more general multiplier test is a conjecture, not a theorem. This supplies a candidate certificate parametrization, not the project's objective-gap constant or an unrestricted converse.

[Fazlyab--Ribeiro--Morari--Preciado, arXiv:1705.03615](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1705.03615), Theorem 3.1 and equations (3.2), (3.5)--(3.9). The framework allows iteration-dependent algorithm matrices and a Lyapunov function containing objective gap and a quadratic state term. Its sufficient matrix conditions include common fixed-point relations, valid quadratic bounds, nonnegative nondecreasing objective weights and PSD storage matrices. The upper guarantee depends on finding those certificates. It does not establish their completeness or the exact minimax profile.

[Carrasco et al., arXiv:1812.02397](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1812.02397), introduction and Definition II.4. This multiplier-search paper treats discrete-time SISO LTI systems. Its FIR approximation/completeness statements must not be promoted to a theorem for arbitrary-dimensional, nonstationary gradient descent. It is background only, not a direct bridge.

## Main's proposed compatibility test, not a source conclusion

The current max4 candidate in research/max4_two_sided_residual_join.md has recovered ordinary ConPP after allowing aggregate-coordinate cancellations while retaining nonnegative actual interpolation coefficients. The remaining difficulty concerns independent finite residual penalties and cross-block information. This is the reason to test a joint terminal storage matrix rather than introduce an unrelated numerical search.

Keep that concrete identity as the starting object. Determine whether a short-memory storage description can retain useful cross terms missing from the two separate squared residual penalties. The required deliverable is an explicit finite-horizon identity with its actual smooth-convex interpolation multipliers and boundary state, or a precise demonstration that the parametrization merely rewrites the already studied cone. A change of vocabulary is not a new route.

At a block join the filter generally has nonzero inherited memory. It cannot be reset to zero to reuse a theorem whose hypothesis was zero initialization. Account for both boundary states and any function-value terms. The algorithm itself remains the original prescribed GD recursion; auxiliary filter states are proof quantities only, never momentum, averaging or adaptive steps. Check the original last-iterate objective normalization, not just an output-norm or stability bound.

The source conjecture is unavailable as a premise. No completeness, time-varying extension, or lossless compression is assumed. If the finite full-interpolation reduction does not provide an actual new join or a stronger exact invariant, record that result and return to the existing quantitative interface. No additional numerical evaluations or routine verifier calls are allocated.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001708 -->
# 0. Mathematical verdict

**Not solved.**

The fixed goal is to decide whether lim_{n -> infinity} W_n^*/U_n^ZJ = 1 for arbitrary predetermined nonnegative schedules, with W_n(h) = sup 2(f(x_n)-f_*)/(L||x_0-x_*||^2) and U_n^ZJ = (2 S_bullet(n)+1)^(-1), using the exact recursions in PROBLEM.md.

There are no signed-closed components in this project. No currently justified proof architecture dominates the others. The main blocker is an all-schedule, all-large-horizon constant-preserving lower bound, or an infinite schedule family with a rigorously smaller worst-case profile. Neither method failure nor proposition failure has been established here. UNKNOWN means no verified evidence, not evidence that the proposed mechanism is impossible.

| Item | Current state |
|---|---|
| Fixed goal | UNCHANGED: decide the exact profile limit, with its original normalization and quantifiers |
| Verified complete proof | NO |
| Verified counterexample | NO |
| Signed-closed sub-tasks | 0 |
| Main blocker | The uniform bound (Q), or a certified asymptotic upper-bound separation |
| Routes marked false/obsolete | NO |
| Highest-priority unresolved task | P0: establish a concrete mechanism that controls the true smooth-convex worst case with leading relative loss tending to zero |

| Sub-task | Status | Closed facts | Conditional facts | Main missing interface |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Probability and legal hard-function construction | UNKNOWN | None | None | Distributional inequality plus finite-dimensional smooth-convex realizability |
| Algebraic / polynomial / finite-field mechanism | UNKNOWN | None | None | Exact link from algebraic object to real legal interpolation and profile |
| Ordered combinatorial decomposition | UNKNOWN | None | None | Constant-preserving relation for arbitrary chronological schedules |
| Strictly improved schedule family | UNKNOWN | None | None | Exact upper-bound certificate and nonvanishing improvement at infinite horizons |
| Complete-candidate adversarial audit | UNKNOWN | None | None | A complete concrete proof object does not yet exist |

Current best proof skeleton (two unresolved alternatives, neither promoted to a theorem):
1. Retain the exact phi/psi dynamic-programming benchmark from PROBLEM.md.
2. For an affirmative result, attach a legal finite-dimensional hard instance to each arbitrary schedule.
3. Establish its objective lower bound without schedule-dependent asymptotic errors.
4. Compare that bound to the exact S_bullet(n) profile for every sufficiently large n.
5. Combine it with the benchmark upper bound after checking the source theorem and normalization.
6. Alternatively, construct an explicit infinite family of legal schedules and exact worst-case upper-bound certificates.
7. Prove a fixed relative separation below the exact benchmark along unbounded horizons.
8. Independently attack a completed argument and verify the surviving full proof.

Central missing lemma: the affirmative route requires, for every epsilon > 0, an N_epsilon independent of h such that for every integer n >= N_epsilon and every h in [0,infinity)^n there is a finite dimension, a convex function with L-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer, and x_0 != x_* for which the normalized objective error is at least (1-epsilon)/(2 S_bullet(n)+1), or a supremum-attainment-free approximation that proves the same W inequality. This is the main theorem-strength obligation itself, not a routine bridge. No reduction currently makes it easier. The negative alternative requires delta>0, n_j -> infinity, and exact certificates W_{n_j}(h^(j)) <= (1-delta) U_{n_j}^ZJ.

# 1. Closed components and obsolete routes

Signed-closed components:

_(no signed-closed components yet)_

Failed or obsolete routes:

_(no failed or obsolete routes recorded; absence here does not mean the strategy is unique)_

The attached paper statements are literature inputs pending exact hypothesis checks; they are not accepted project facts. Earlier local research concerned a weaker exponent target. Its historical warnings are useful for avoiding unjustified reductions but are not imported facts.

# 2. Interface contract table

## Legal hard-function realization — connect proposed data to the problem
Input required: real finite-dimensional points, function values and gradients satisfying every required convex L-smooth interpolation inequality, a valid minimizer, and the exact GD recursion for the supplied h.
Output claimed: a legal member of the problem's function class giving a lower bound on W_n(h).
Available facts: none.
Missing verification on the actual model: candidate data, PSD/realization, normalization, and objective value.
Failure mode if ignored: formal matrices or probabilistic data need not be realizable by a single global function.
Status: UNKNOWN.

## Exact profile comparison — preserve the leading constant
Input required: a realizable all-schedule bound and the exact discrete sequences S_circle and S_bullet.
Output claimed: a schedule-uniform multiplicative loss tending to zero relative to U_n^ZJ for all large horizons.
Available facts: none.
Missing verification on the actual model: loss estimates uniform in h, non-dyadic horizons, zero and arbitrarily large steps.
Failure mode if ignored: an exponent bound, a subpolynomial loss or a restricted family can be mistaken for the target.
Status: UNKNOWN.

## Negative schedule certificate — disprove the profile claim
Input required: explicit nonnegative predetermined schedules and exact full-class upper-bound certificates.
Output claimed: a fixed relative improvement at an unbounded sequence of horizons.
Available facts: none.
Missing verification on the actual model: exact certificate feasibility, legal GD-only dynamics, amplification and comparison with the actual benchmark sequence.
Failure mode if ignored: finite-horizon numerical improvement need not survive at infinity.
Status: UNKNOWN.

# 3. Dangerous heuristic lines and strategies not to pursue

- "A scalar functional satisfies the same concatenation recursion." Conditional only: the source proposes this as a possible mechanism. A relation to arbitrary schedules and legal hard instances must precede any universal conclusion.
- "Optimizing the existing checkpoint parameters removes the loss." Not automatic: the supplied source warns that auxiliary constants deteriorate as parameters shrink. An exponent correction cannot be hidden inside a constant-level o(1).
- "0.4239 is the asymptotic constant." Conflated with a stronger claim: the target is the exact benchmark profile; the decimal is a numerical estimate.
- "A polynomial or finite-field identity proves the lower bound." Conditional only: a real inequality, PSD/realizability and a uniform quantitative comparison remain necessary.
- "A smaller finite-n PEP value disproves (P)." Conflated with a stronger claim: a nonvanishing separation on infinitely many unbounded horizons is required.
- Do not erase chronological cross-state data before optimizing a structural bound.
- Do not treat source claims, shared attempts or old replay counts as verifier-accepted facts.
- Do not assume bounded, ordered, positive or recursively composable schedules.
- Do not confuse a feasible PEP dual upper bound with a lower-bound hard instance.
- Do not call a theorem-equivalent missing minimax lemma routine.

# 4. Missing bridge lemmas

## Bridge 1 — Candidate realization (independent discovery obligation)
Target statement: for a proposed family of interpolation data indexed by (n,h), all the smooth-convex interpolation inequalities and the exact GD equations hold in finite dimension, with a valid normalized minimizer.
Prerequisites: a fully specified candidate data family; none exists yet.
Existing facts to use: none.
Missing checks: explicit data, PSD or constructive embedding, global function realization, normalized objective value.
Closure criterion: a complete proof for the actual candidate with all schedule-dependent parameters explicit. This is an interface contract, not an asserted lemma.

## Bridge 2 — Loss accounting (dependent on a concrete construction)
Target statement: for a concrete candidate bound B_n(h), inf_{h in [0,infinity)^n} B_n(h)/U_n^ZJ >= 1-eta_n with eta_n >= 0 and eta_n -> 0.
Prerequisites: B_n(h) <= W_n(h) established by a legal construction.
Existing facts to use: none.
Missing checks: candidate definition, uniform constants and all-horizon asymptotics.
Closure criterion: an actual derivation avoiding assumptions equivalent to the desired inequality. As written this obligation is theorem-strength and cannot count as progress.

## Bridge 3 — Certified improvement propagation (independent alternative)
Target statement: for a concrete recursively specified family h^(j) of lengths n_j -> infinity, an exact certificate proves W_{n_j}(h^(j)) <= (1-delta) U_{n_j}^ZJ for one explicit delta>0.
Prerequisites: a concrete seed, propagation rule and valid upper-bound potential.
Existing facts to use: none.
Missing checks: exact feasibility, GD-only implementation, asymptotic nonvanishing gain and benchmark comparison.
Closure criterion: a complete family proof. An improved seed alone leaves this interface open.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001709 -->
# Source lead: coefficient signs and dual arc diagrams

Primary sources checked September27,2026. This note is awareness/strategy,
not a verifier-accepted theorem for the project.

## General certificate congruence still needs signs

Shu--Wang, [A Unified Theory of H-Duality in First-Order Methods](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.03281v1), Sections1.1--1.2,4.3--4.7 and AppendixC.

The paper transforms certificate matrices so that the associated full slack
forms are congruent. The remaining coefficient-sign conditions are explicit;
PSD preservation alone is insufficient. Proposition2 supplies sign preservation
for invertible reduced tridiagonal certificates in the listed D/G performance
pairings. The main text also points to a combinatorial characterization in the
source below. This does not establish reversal of an arbitrary intrinsic V_h
certificate: its performance setup, actual signs and any certificate selection
must still be matched. The main agent separately confirmed the scope audit
already published as4ad2db6604a59a2c.

## A precise combinatorial sign criterion in another setting

Yoon--Grimmer, [A Theory of Composition and Duality of Extremal Optimal Fixed-Point Algorithms](https://arxiv.org/html/2605.02231v2), v2 August6,2026, Sections3--4, especially Theorem4 and its proof in4.2.2.

The setting is minimax-optimal vertex algorithms for nonexpansive fixed-point
residuals. Their interpolation-multiplier supports are encoded by arc diagrams.
Theorem4 says the antitransposed vertex method remains optimal exactly when
its arc diagram is noncrossing; the resulting method is again a vertex. The
proof relates signs of finite differences of an inverse certificate matrix to
weighted graph/forest structure. Crossing examples in this class show why a
generic appeal to time reversal is insufficient. The theorem is not about
arbitrary predetermined GD schedules or the intrinsic primitive energy.

## Concrete applicability test, not an imported conclusion

For max4's proposed general reversal interface, the possible lead is to seek
a sign-controlling structure in the ACTUAL intrinsic certificate array, or an
exact obstruction to a proposed matrix transform. First match the performance
functional, row/column constraints, reduction/invertibility and the distinction
between a given certificate and all certificates for the schedule. Failure of
one transformed certificate does not disprove existence of another certificate.
An optimal-vertex assumption cannot be imposed on the current problem without
proof. Likewise, no noncrossing or decomposition hypothesis may be presumed.

These are DUAL multiplier graphs. C35/C39's primal coordinate-flow graph
obstructions do not automatically exclude this proof architecture. Conversely,
the source's vertex classification does not validate those primal constructions.
This is an optional mechanism lead inside the current task, with no additional
numerical, verification, model or round allocation.

<!-- END_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001709 -->

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001710 -->
# Two scoped primary-source leads

Read on September27,2026. These are literature leads, not accepted Danus facts.
The original exact all-horizon objective target is unchanged.

## Controlled repair and inherited-component interference

Liu--Ye, [Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal for Gradient Descent: The Strongly Convex Case](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.26873v1), v1 September22,2026.

Source scope: Theorem1.1 and Corollary1.2 concern strong convexity, relative
distance/function reduction and a subpolynomial condition-number loss. They do
not prove the convex exact constant/profile target. Sections2.2,3.2--3.3,4.2
and5.2 track activation level relative to remaining amplitude. The repair
construction controls this ratio; Lemma3.3 inserts a finite triangle-envelope
bridge that tolerates a nonnegative contribution from the previous component,
then removes interference in the next component's input coordinate. Complete
inactivity of an older component is not required. The stated bridge assumes
kappa>=4, small gap mass relative to kappa, positive available amplitude and
checkpoint size greater than4. Its transfer pays an explicit constant loss.

Main inference to test, not a theorem from this paper: the geometric lane may
benefit from tolerating a controlled inherited gradient rather than demanding
zero contribution everywhere. First compare this with the actual retained
Phi/Psi/Dist/G/Xi interface. A direct citation cannot remove the constant loss
or import a strong-convex limit into the current convex family. If the number
of repairs grows or the loss already defeats the near-critical scale, record
that exact failure and do not rebuild the exponent proof.

## Graph-structured full slack certificates

Bok--Altschuler, [Accelerating Proximal Gradient Descent via Silver Stepsizes](https://optimization-online.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/proxgd_stepsize_accel.pdf).

Source scope: Sections1.2 and3, Theorem3.1, Definition3.5 and Lemma3.6 concern
the specified silver proximal-GD construction. Two sets of nonnegative
interpolation multipliers and a full slack matrix certify multi-step descent.
After taking a Schur complement, the relevant matrix has Laplacian structure;
nonpositive off-diagonal entries and zero row sums give its PSD certificate.
The construction is recursive and does not establish completeness for arbitrary
GD certificates, general signed-terminal-credit re-selection, or minimax
optimality of arbitrary schedules. Its sign convention for interpolation is
opposite to this project's Q<=0 convention.

Main inference to test, not a result of the source: max4's re-selection problem
could be screened for an actual graph/Schur-complement repair which preserves
value flow and every coefficient of the full gradient matrix. A PSD graph
identity alone does not repair negative coefficients on actual interpolation
inequalities. The existing tight intrinsic-memory rigidity still applies to
its stated class; merely adding positive quadratic memory is not a new route.

## Dispatch boundary

These leads supplement the current concrete tasks; they do not replace the
near-critical diagnostic, renew solver/verification allowances, extend round
caps or authorize new model types. Use the sources only after checking their
actual hypotheses, and publish the result of the applicability test in shared
evidence. No external theorem has been added to the fact graph.

<!-- END_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001710 -->

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001711 -->
# Exact Constant Matching Lower Bound for Predetermined-Step Gradient Descent

Resolve the following minimax problem completely.

The objective is not merely to recover the silver exponent. The goal is to determine whether the best possible predetermined nonnegative stepsize schedule for gradient descent matches, asymptotically and with the correct leading constant/profile, the objective-value schedule constructed by Zehao Zhang and Rujun Jiang.

Throughout, “complexity” means the convergence/iteration-complexity rate as a function of the number of gradient steps \(n\), not the computational complexity of constructing a stepsize schedule.

---

## 1. Optimization model

Let \(L>0\), and let \(\mathcal C^{1,1}_L\) denote the class of all finite-dimensional convex differentiable functions

\[
f:\mathbb R^d\to\mathbb R
\]

whose gradient is \(L\)-Lipschitz and which possess at least one minimizer \(x_\star\).

For a predetermined nonnegative stepsize schedule

\[
h=(h_0,\ldots,h_{n-1})\in[0,\infty)^n,
\]

gradient descent is

\[
x_{t+1}
=
x_t-\frac{h_t}{L}\nabla f(x_t),
\qquad
t=0,\ldots,n-1.
\]

The schedule may depend on the horizon \(n\), but it must be chosen before seeing \(f\), \(x_0\), gradients, function values, or any other oracle information.

Define the normalized worst-case objective error

\[
\mathcal W_n(h)
:=
\sup
\frac{2\bigl(f(x_n)-f_\star\bigr)}
{L\|x_0-x_\star\|^2},
\]

where the supremum is over

- every finite dimension \(d\);
- every \(f\in\mathcal C^{1,1}_L\);
- every minimizer \(x_\star\in\arg\min f\);
- every \(x_0\neq x_\star\).

Define the minimax value

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal W_n^\star
:=
\inf_{h\in[0,\infty)^n}\mathcal W_n(h).
}
\]

The normalization is chosen so that it agrees directly with the constants in Zhang–Jiang.

If instead one uses the normalization

\[
\mathcal R_n(h)
=
\sup
\frac{f(x_n)-f_\star}{L\|x_0-x_\star\|^2},
\]

then

\[
\mathcal W_n(h)=2\mathcal R_n(h).
\]

Do not mix the two normalizations.

---

## 2. The Zhang–Jiang benchmark

Write

\[
\rho
:=
\log_2(1+\sqrt2)
=
1.271553303\ldots.
\]

Define

\[
\varphi(x,y)
=
\frac{
-x-y+\sqrt{(x+y+2)^2+4(x+1)(y+1)}
}{2},
\]

and

\[
\psi(x,y)
=
\frac{
3-2y+\sqrt{(2y+1)(2y+8x+9)}
}{4}.
\]

Define two scalar sequences recursively.

First,

\[
S_\circ(0)=0,
\]

and for every \(n\ge1\),

\[
\boxed{
S_\circ(n)
=
\max_{0\le k<n}
\Bigl[
S_\circ(k)
+
\varphi\!\bigl(S_\circ(k),S_\circ(n-k-1)\bigr)
+
S_\circ(n-k-1)
\Bigr].
}
\]

Second,

\[
S_\bullet(0)=0,
\]

and for every \(n\ge1\),

\[
\boxed{
S_\bullet(n)
=
\max_{0\le k<n}
\Bigl[
S_\circ(k)
+
\psi\!\bigl(S_\circ(k),S_\bullet(n-k-1)\bigr)
+
S_\bullet(n-k-1)
\Bigr].
}
\]

These are the sums of the primitive and dominant schedules generated by the Zhang–Jiang concatenation dynamic program.

Let \(h_\bullet^{(n)}\) be any Zhang–Jiang dominant schedule attaining \(S_\bullet(n)\).

Their analysis gives the exact worst-case objective profile

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal W_n\!\left(h_\bullet^{(n)}\right)
=
\frac{1}{2S_\bullet(n)+1}.
}
\]

The equality is important: the associated Huber instance attains the bound.

Define

\[
\boxed{
U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}
:=
\frac{1}{2S_\bullet(n)+1}.
}
\]

Consequently,

\[
\mathcal W_n^\star
\le
U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}
\qquad
\text{for every }n.
\]

This exact sequence, rather than the decimal \(0.4239\), is the benchmark that must be matched.

---

## 3. Exact problem to resolve

Determine whether

\[
\boxed{
\lim_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\mathcal W_n^\star}
{U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}}
=
1.
}
\tag{P}
\]

Equivalently, determine whether

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal W_n^\star
\ge
\frac{1-o(1)}
{2S_\bullet(n)+1}.
}
\tag{LB}
\]

Since the reverse inequality

\[
\mathcal W_n^\star
\le
\frac{1}{2S_\bullet(n)+1}
\]

is already supplied by the Zhang–Jiang schedule, proving (LB) establishes asymptotic minimax optimality of their schedule horizon by horizon.

The quantifiers in (LB) are strict:

for every \(\varepsilon>0\), there must exist \(N_\varepsilon<\infty\) such that for every \(n\ge N_\varepsilon\) and every predetermined schedule \(h\in[0,\infty)^n\),

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal W_n(h)
\ge
\frac{1-\varepsilon}
{2S_\bullet(n)+1}.
}
\tag{Q}
\]

The threshold \(N_\varepsilon\) must be independent of the particular schedule \(h\).

The adversarial function and its dimension may depend on \(n\) and on \(h\).

---

## 4. What “matching the complexity and constant” means

A complete affirmative solution must prove (P), not merely the silver exponent.

In particular, since

\[
S_\bullet(n)=\Theta(n^\rho),
\]

(P) automatically implies the optimal polynomial convergence exponent

\[
\rho=\log_2(1+\sqrt2).
\]

It also matches the complete asymptotic constant profile, including any genuine oscillation of \(S_\bullet(n)/n^\rho\).

For reference, define

\[
\bar\nu_\bullet
:=
\liminf_{n\to\infty}
\frac{S_\bullet(n)}{n^\rho}
\]

and

\[
C_{\mathrm{ZJ}}
:=
\frac{1}{2\bar\nu_\bullet}.
\]

Then

\[
\limsup_{n\to\infty}
n^\rho U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}
=
C_{\mathrm{ZJ}}.
\]

Therefore (P) implies

\[
\boxed{
\limsup_{n\to\infty}
n^\rho\mathcal W_n^\star
=
C_{\mathrm{ZJ}}.
}
\]

However, proving only this limsup identity is weaker than (P) and does **not** count as a complete affirmative solution.

Likewise, proving merely

\[
\mathcal W_n^\star=\Theta(n^{-\rho})
\]

does not count.

---

## 5. Important warning about the number \(0.4239\)

Do not formulate the theorem as

\[
\mathcal W_n^\star
\sim
0.4239\,n^{-\rho}.
\]

That is not the precise Zhang–Jiang theorem.

Their rigorous asymptotic statement is expressed through

\[
\bar\nu_\bullet
=
\liminf_{n\to\infty}
S_\bullet(n)/n^\rho
\]

and the coefficient

\[
(2\bar\nu_\bullet)^{-1}.
\]

The frequently quoted number \(0.4239\) comes from an additional numerical estimate for \(\bar\nu_\bullet\), and is not itself the exact rigorously identified asymptotic constant.

The paper also notes numerical evidence for still smaller constants.

Thus:

- \(0.4239\) may be used as a numerical sanity check;
- it may not be assumed as an exact constant;
- a proof that only matches \(0.4239\) numerically is not sufficient;
- the rigorous benchmark is \(U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}=(2S_\bullet(n)+1)^{-1}\).

---

## 6. Current lower-bound benchmark

A recent lower bound of Ye and Liu considers, after setting \(L=1\),

\[
\mathcal R_n(h)
=
\sup_{f,x_0}
\frac{f(x_n)-f_\star}
{\|x_0-x_\star\|^2},
\qquad
r_n^\star
=
\inf_{h\ge0}\mathcal R_n(h),
\]

and proves that for some absolute \(C>0\),

\[
r_n^\star
\ge
n^{-\left(
\rho+
C\sqrt{\frac{\log\log n}{\log n}}
\right)}
\]

for all sufficiently large \(n\).

Equivalently, in the normalization of this problem,

\[
\mathcal W_n^\star
\ge
2
n^{-\left(
\rho+
C\sqrt{\frac{\log\log n}{\log n}}
\right)}.
\]

This establishes the correct polynomial exponent only up to a subpolynomial loss.

It does **not** establish (P).

Removing the exponent loss alone is also not sufficient: the leading asymptotic constant/profile must be matched.

---

## 7. Existing hard-function tool

The Ye–Liu lower bound may be used as a starting point.

For checkpoints

\[
1\le t_1<\cdots<t_k\le n,
\]

write

\[
b_i=h_{t_i},
\qquad
s_i
=
\sum_{j=t_{i-1}+1}^{t_i-1}h_j,
\]

with \(t_0=0\), and let \(s_{k+1}\) denote the sum of the steps after the last checkpoint.

For suitable parameters \(\epsilon,\gamma>0\), putting

\[
c=\sqrt{1+2\gamma+\epsilon^2},
\]

their construction yields constants \(a=a(\epsilon,\gamma)\) and \(B_0=B_0(\epsilon)\) such that, when every checkpoint obeys \(b_i\ge B_0\),

\[
\mathcal R_n(h)
\ge
\frac{1}{4(1+s_{k+1})}
\prod_{i=1}^k
\left[
\frac{b_i}
{c(a+s_i)+\epsilon b_i}
\right]^2.
\]

Hence

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal W_n(h)
\ge
\frac{1}{2(1+s_{k+1})}
\prod_{i=1}^k
\left[
\frac{b_i}
{c(a+s_i)+\epsilon b_i}
\right]^2.
}
\tag{Y--L}
\]

You may sharpen, replace, or completely abandon this construction.

Do not assume that merely optimizing its existing parameters is sufficient.

A central obstruction in the current proof is that the auxiliary constant \(a(\epsilon,\gamma)\) deteriorates rapidly when parameters are sent toward the regime needed to recover the exact silver exponent. Any proposed constant-level proof must track such losses quantitatively and cannot hide them inside \(o(1)\) unless that \(o(1)\) is genuinely negligible relative to \(U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}\).

---

## 8. Acceptable complete resolutions

Assume for purposes of this task that the question admits a rigorous resolution, but do not assume in advance that the answer to (P) is affirmative.

### Affirmative resolution

Prove

\[
\lim_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\mathcal W_n^\star}
{U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}}
=
1.
\]

Because the upper bound is known, the substantive requirement is the uniform lower bound (Q).

A complete proof must apply to every sufficiently large horizon \(n\) and every predetermined nonnegative schedule of length \(n\).

### Negative resolution

Prove rigorously that (P) is false.

For example, it is sufficient to establish that there exist

\[
\delta>0,
\qquad
n_j\to\infty,
\qquad
h^{(j)}\in[0,\infty)^{n_j},
\]

such that

\[
\mathcal W_{n_j}\!\left(h^{(j)}\right)
\le
(1-\delta)
U_{n_j}^{\mathrm{ZJ}}
\]

for every \(j\), with a rigorous worst-case upper-bound certificate.

Equivalently, it is sufficient to prove

\[
\liminf_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\mathcal W_n^\star}
{U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}}
<1.
\]

Numerical evidence is not a negative resolution.

Failure to prove the lower bound is not evidence that it is false.

---

## 9. Results that do not count as a solution

Do **not** stop for any of the following:

- reproducing the known \(n^{-\rho-o(1)}\) lower bound;
- proving \(n^{-\rho-\varepsilon}\) for every fixed \(\varepsilon>0\);
- proving only that \(\rho\) is the optimal polynomial exponent;
- proving \(\mathcal W_n^\star=\Theta(n^{-\rho})\);
- obtaining an unspecified positive constant times \(n^{-\rho}\);
- obtaining a constant strictly smaller than the Zhang–Jiang benchmark;
- proving the desired constant only along \(n=2^m-1\);
- proving it along any other subsequence;
- proving only a limsup or liminf lower bound;
- proving only
  \[
  \limsup n^\rho\mathcal W_n^\star=C_{\mathrm{ZJ}};
  \]
- showing that a candidate hard instance works for the silver schedule but not for every schedule;
- restricting stepsizes to a bounded interval;
- assuming stepsizes are monotone, ordered, separated, or bounded away from zero;
- assuming the schedule has a prescribed recursive/composable form;
- assuming all large steps can be selected as checkpoints without proving this;
- proving a lower bound only for Zhang–Jiang's class of concatenated schedules;
- proving a lower bound only for “basic”, “composable”, periodic, symmetric, or silver-like schedules;
- replacing nonnegative schedules by positive schedules without treating zero steps;
- permitting the adversary to choose the schedule after seeing the function;
- allowing adaptive stepsizes, momentum, averaging, restarting, or additional state;
- proving a result for gradient norm rather than last-iterate objective gap;
- solving the anytime version instead of the finite-horizon non-anytime problem;
- changing the performance normalization;
- dropping the factor \(1/2\) in the Zhang–Jiang normalization;
- treating \(0.4239\) as an exact proven constant;
- using floating-point calculations as proof of an asymptotic constant;
- producing a stronger lower bound than the Zhang–Jiang upper bound without noticing the resulting contradiction;
- hiding a subpolynomial loss inside a claimed constant;
- obtaining a bound of the form
  \[
  n^{-\rho}\exp[-o(\log n)]
  \]
  rather than
  \[
  (1-o(1))U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}};
  \]
- proving finite-\(n\) inequalities for finitely many computed horizons;
- providing only a conjectured extremal schedule;
- providing only a conjectured hard function;
- reducing the problem to an unproved statement of essentially the same minimax strength;
- reporting a promising lemma, numerical pattern, or “substantial progress”.

Intermediate positive progress is not a stopping condition.

---

## 10. Proof standards

Every invoked theorem must be stated with the hypotheses needed here.

In particular, carefully verify:

- convexity;
- \(L\)-smoothness;
- existence of a minimizer;
- the exact GD recursion;
- nonnegativity of all stepsizes;
- the distinction between a schedule chosen for a fixed horizon and an infinite anytime schedule;
- the normalization by \(L\|x_0-x_\star\|^2/2\);
- uniformity over every schedule \(h\in[0,\infty)^n\);
- whether constants are absolute or parameter dependent;
- every use of \(o(1)\), including its sign and uniformity;
- every limit interchange;
- every passage between \(\mathcal R_n\) and \(\mathcal W_n\);
- every use of finite-dimensional interpolation;
- every claim that a constructed collection of values and gradients is realizable by a convex \(L\)-smooth function.

A lower-bound construction may depend on \(n\) and on the entire predetermined schedule \(h\), because the worst-case function is chosen after the schedule.

Its dimension may grow with \(n\).

---

## 11. Promising proof families to investigate

Use genuinely different approaches rather than repeatedly varying one incomplete argument.

Relevant families include:

- exact smooth-convex interpolation and primal PEP constructions;
- dual PEP certificates and complementary slackness;
- deriving a dual dynamic program to the Zhang–Jiang concatenation recursion;
- minimax/Bellman formulations in which \(\varphi\) and \(\psi\) arise from primal-dual optimality;
- hard functions whose recursive composition exactly mirrors ConPP/ConPD;
- sharper checkpoint constructions without the \(\epsilon\)-dependent constant explosion;
- curved-gradient or Moreau-envelope constructions with exact rather than approximate transfer factors;
- piecewise quadratic or Huber-type interpolation constructions;
- finite-dimensional resisting-oracle constructions;
- composition/duality of extremal first-order methods;
- exact characterization of the PEP value for arbitrary nonnegative stepsizes;
- identification of a scalar functional \(V(h)\) satisfying both a universal lower bound and the same concatenation recursion as \(S_\bullet\);
- structural reductions proving that an arbitrary schedule can be decomposed at essentially no constant loss;
- induction on \(n\) with a sharp split inequality;
- continuous scaling limits of the recursion, but only when converted back to rigorous discrete estimates with the correct constant;
- exact algebraic or semialgebraic certificates for small horizons that reveal the correct general recursion.

Do not assume one of these approaches must succeed.

---

## 12. Multiagent search protocol

Use multiagent v2 aggressively and dynamically, with up to four concurrent agents if available.

Begin with a genuinely diverse portfolio.

Do not tell most early agents what the currently favored proof route is.

Maintain an explicit registry of mathematical approach families, organized by actual mechanism rather than wording.

If multiple agents converge to the same checkpoint construction, PEP dual, or recurrence argument, redirect some agents to genuinely different formulations.

Keep both possibilities alive:

1. the Zhang–Jiang schedule is asymptotically minimax optimal;
2. another schedule beats its constant/profile.

Do not discard the second possibility merely because the exponents match.

When an approach reaches a missing lemma whose strength is essentially equivalent to (Q), mark the route as blocked. Do not call that lemma “technical” or “routine”.

Only reopen such a route if a new mechanism appears.

---

## 13. Computational agents

Use computation as an adversarial and discovery tool, not as proof.

For moderate \(n\), agents should attempt to compute or tightly bound:

\[
\mathcal W_n^\star,
\qquad
U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}},
\qquad
\frac{\mathcal W_n^\star}{U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}},
\]

using exact or high-precision PEP/SDP/branch-and-bound methods where possible.

They should also:

- compute \(S_\circ(n)\) and \(S_\bullet(n)\);
- inspect the maximizing split indices;
- search for schedules beating \(h_\bullet^{(n)}\);
- test proposed universal hard-function inequalities;
- search for counterexamples to intermediate lemmas;
- inspect primal and dual PEP optimizers;
- identify rank patterns and active interpolation constraints;
- attempt rational/algebraic reconstruction of numerical certificates;
- verify normalization factors independently.

A numerical counterexample to a proposed lemma is useful and should kill that lemma.

A numerical confirmation of the main conjecture is not a proof.

---

## 14. Adversarial audit

Every proposed complete affirmative proof must be independently attacked for at least the following failure modes:

- it actually proves only the exponent;
- a fixed \(\varepsilon>0\) remains in the exponent;
- \(\varepsilon=\varepsilon_n\to0\) creates a hidden constant blowup;
- it proves only infinitely many horizons;
- it proves only dyadic horizons;
- its \(o(1)\) depends on the schedule;
- its hard function is not globally convex and smooth;
- the interpolation data are inconsistent;
- the minimizer used in the normalization is not valid;
- the dimension is implicitly infinite without justification;
- very large stepsizes are excluded;
- zero stepsizes break the argument;
- a checkpoint-selection lemma silently loses a constant;
- a product inequality loses a nonvanishing factor;
- a factor \(2\) is lost when translating between normalizations;
- the Zhang–Jiang constant is replaced by \(0.4239\);
- a limsup statement is mistaken for horizon-by-horizon asymptotics;
- a numerical observation is used inside a supposedly analytic proof;
- the proof establishes optimality only within a restricted schedule family;
- a PEP dual certificate is feasible only numerically rather than exactly;
- strong duality is invoked without verifying its hypotheses;
- an asymptotic recurrence is substituted for the exact discrete one;
- the final lower bound exceeds a known rigorous upper bound because of a normalization error.

A proof survives only if all such issues are resolved explicitly.

---

## 15. Output requirements

Do not return an exploratory essay.

Do not return a research diary.

Do not return a collection of partial lemmas.

Do not return a “most promising approach”.

Do not return merely because several approaches fail.

Do not return merely because agents agree that the problem is difficult.

Do not return a positive intermediate result.

Return only one of the following:

### A. Complete affirmative proof

A rigorous proof of

\[
\lim_{n\to\infty}
\frac{\mathcal W_n^\star}
{U_n^{\mathrm{ZJ}}}
=
1,
\]

including every necessary lemma and every constant/normalization check.

### B. Complete negative proof

A rigorous proof that the above limit is not \(1\), with an explicit asymptotic separation or another logically conclusive disproof.

Before returning either answer, subject the argument to an independent adversarial audit and repair every identified gap.

If the argument still contains an unproved lemma of theorem-level strength, the problem is not resolved and you must continue working.

Do not label a missing minimax inequality, uniformity statement, interpolation theorem, structural decomposition, or constant-preserving recursion as “routine”.

The stopping criterion is mathematical completion, not positive progress.

---

## 16. Literature usage

The following sources may be treated as background starting points, but their statements and normalizations must be checked carefully:

- Zehao Zhang and Rujun Jiang, *Accelerated Gradient Descent by Concatenation of Stepsize Schedules*, arXiv:2410.12395 / SIAM Journal on Optimization.
- Yuhan Ye and Kaizhao Liu, *Silver Rate Is (Almost) Optimal for Gradient Descent*, arXiv:2609.09152 and the accompanying lower-bound exposition.
- standard smooth-convex interpolation and PEP results;
- standard results concerning the silver stepsize schedule and concatenation constructions.

Public search may be used for standard named theorems, definitions, and the cited papers.

Do not use public search merely to locate a posted solution to this exact benchmark.

The task is to produce and verify the mathematical resolution itself.

# Probability and adversarial geometry

Read the project `../../PROBLEM.md` completely and `/opt/danus/runtime/imports/gd-exact-profile-20260926/DISPATCH_POLICY.md`. The exact profile comparison (P) is the fixed goal; both answers remain possible. You are the probability/adversarial-geometry route, not a verifier.

Investigate a genuinely probabilistic mechanism for arbitrary predetermined nonnegative schedules: schedule-dependent distributions on finite-dimensional legal hard functions, random embeddings or orthogonal directions, multiscale adversarial branching, martingale/energy arguments, or an information-based obstruction. Derive an actual finite-dimensional interpolation construction or a precise realizability contract. A distributional bound must preserve the supremum/expectation direction and normalized initial distance. Do not invoke a minimax interchange without proving its hypotheses.

Start by choosing and developing a mechanism independently of other workers' favored routes. Your first concrete deliverable is a fully specified candidate distribution/construction and its exact expected objective inequality, or an exact obstruction showing why that mechanism cannot match the profile. If it fails, move to a materially different probabilistic construction, not cosmetic checkpoint parameter changes. A proof of a positive lower-bound constant smaller than the benchmark is only partial.

Do not stop on a central lemma equivalent to the original minimax claim: mark that route BLOCKED, write the exact quantifiers of the missing statement to shared memory, and search a new mechanism. Keep chronology and full smooth-convex interpolation constraints; high-dimensional randomness does not automatically establish PSD or function existence. A complete affirmative result must have a threshold independent of h and handle every large n, zero steps, and unbounded steps.

Use `gm_add` to publish substantive attempts and obstructions as you go, with route mechanism, exact scope, candidate formula, and evidence label. Shared artifacts may be written under the project's `research/` directory with your own filename prefix; link their paths in shared memory. Follow the selective-verification policy: do not spend the wave repeatedly verifying routine lemmas. Never fabricate fact IDs, hand-edit truth stores, or invoke other model names. Read new assignments and shared guidance at round boundaries. Keep working toward a full resolution through genuinely different routes; do not end merely because one candidate fails.

# Polynomial and finite-field methods

Read the project `../../PROBLEM.md` completely and `/opt/danus/runtime/imports/gd-exact-profile-20260926/DISPATCH_POLICY.md`. The exact profile comparison (P) is the fixed goal; both answers remain possible. You own algebraic and polynomial mechanisms.

Investigate schedule polynomials, moment problems, orthogonal polynomial or extremal polynomial inequalities, noncommutative products representing changing curvature, and finite-field/combinatorial designs that might produce legal real Gram matrices or force rank/energy constraints. Begin independently rather than repeating another worker's checkpoint ansatz. Identify the specific extra information beyond a scalar quadratic polynomial that is needed to capture all convex smooth functions.

Your first concrete deliverable is a precise algebraic object attached to every real nonnegative schedule and a proved connection to legal smooth-convex interpolation, or an exact counterexample to a proposed connection. If finite fields are used, supply an explicit real lift and prove the relevant PSD, normalization, and GD realization properties. Congruence, finite-field nonvanishing, or a formal polynomial identity alone cannot prove a real analytic inequality. Keep arbitrary real step sizes; do not restrict to rational or algebraic schedules without a uniform approximation argument.

Seek a quantitative mechanism matching the exact discrete S_bullet profile, or one that supports a rigorously better upper-bound family. Quadratic-only lower bounds and finitely many exact PEP certificates are exploratory until the all-schedule, all-horizon bridge is proved. A missing lifting or comparison theorem of minimax strength makes that route BLOCKED. Record the obstruction, then change mechanisms rather than repackaging it.

Use `gm_add` for substantive discoveries, proof attempts, and failures, naming mechanism, precise claim, normalization, and evidence status. Shared artifacts may be placed in the project's `research/` directory with your own filename prefix and linked from shared memory. Follow the selective-verification policy; submit only a completed target-specific bridge or a complete target proof, initially at most one selected package. Never hand-edit truth stores or invoke any model other than gpt-6-astra/max. Keep going within each authorized round and check new guidance at round boundaries.
